We have over 200 000 children in Austria who visit childcare facilities until the sixth year of life. During the summertime, 180,000 of them stand in front of locked doors! This is similar in every federal country: Lock all childcare facilities in Burgenland. In Lower Austria, 1 029 kindergartens are closed up, there is only something that remains open more than 1 percent, and they are not really open all the time. I have many letters mainly from working mothers who do not know how to reconcile the holiday period with 25 holiday weeks with their professional activity. They say it's different in Lower Austria. But a mother from Brunn at the mountain range says that only one of three kindergartens is open, and only during the first and last week of the holiday, and that she does not know how to manage it at all. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] This is a concrete problem where it really does not make great circumstances to find a solution. There are many families who have several jobs, but cannot live on them anymore. But they are now moving the whole issue to the next election, to the next government negotiations. ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-09-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00067_d8e11799	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! There is today a proposal for a law aimed at a general ban on smoking in gastronomy establishments. This affects a total of at least 60 000 restaurants in Austria, coffee houses, innhouses. In addition, there are also box hams, events where the administration of food and drinks is allowed, tent-fests and so on. So that's something that's more worrying. I think we agree that smoking is very harmful to health. We don't need to stress this here, as I mean. But we don't solve the problem with a ban on smoking in gastronomy companies. The real problem is: For example, in the home area much bigger. Living together with a partner who smokes is far more harmful than visiting a local. I am still visiting a local voluntarily, so I am deliberately putting myself there at risk of my health. That is why I believe that this law really goes beyond the real problem. It will not lead any people to abandon his cigarette. No one will stop smoking. That is why, in my opinion, this law is a bit of a self-evident and an alibrition. We would be better advised and we would be more sensible to take measures to help smokers get rid of their vices, to get away from their addiction. And it is no secret that the most common New Year's plans of the Austrians are to stop smoking each year. And every year, tens of thousands of Austrians fail in exactly this project and are back on their problem. Therefore, it would be far more sensible if one honestly meant that we should make people in this country healthier and really want to remove them from smoking, to do the following: Let us take the tobacco tax, which is not very low, and let us make a commitment to this tobacco tax, and let us take it back to these people by offering them treatment options that they can really get away from smoking by giving them support by providing assistance to these people as well. I don't think it makes sense to have a smoker hate here by following smokers with great punishments almost and trying with great punishment to get them away from smoking. A positive motivation is a far more sensible, far better and far more successful. And finally, it is what is our common goal. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-09-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00067_d8e11865	Thank you, Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Today's novel to the Tobacco Act is another important step towards health protection, in my opinion. We started in 1996 when we first, then with Minister Krammer, tried to enforce a tobacco law. At that time, the starting point was quite different from today, then the tobacco industry challenged the fact that smoking is harmful at all. We have achieved in the public sector that small rooms have been made smoke-free, very small inscriptions. That was the start. Since then, much has been done, first worldwide, second Europe-wide, but thirdly also in Austria. The last step was the voluntary agreement between the Federal Minister of Smoking and Gastronomy. And you see it every day, there's hardly a local where there's no non-smoking zone. There is such a way, I would like to say, a peaceful side-by-side, with the “peaceful” already being marked, some smokers are quite careless. Today's law is turning to a small circle, which is to say gastronomy, because believing that the tobacco problem is solved if smoking is less or smoking is not exposed is a mistake. The danger is mainly caused by the self-smokering. The passive smoking is probably at least ten times less dangerous, I would not say. If you look at WHO numbers, you'll see that smokers die eight years earlier and 30 percent of all – everyone! – Carcinomas – which would be 11 000 in Austria – are caused by smoking, 85 percent of COPD, where people suffocate painfully, 85 percent of throat cancer and lung cancer caused by smoking. So it must go our focus once toward active smoking. In passive smoking, the main damage factor is found in the family environment. Ask the children who are then suffering from asthma or who have a worse development of the lung, who are passively tormented in the home room. That means you have to sort the problem out once. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: All who are not here right now are smoking!]] But also in the gastronomy, protection of non-smokers is important, and I think this path is a reasonable step. I know there are attempts abroad to produce a kind of total non-smoker protection in the gastronomy. Firstly, as I said earlier, it is rather blue-eyed, because it does not solve the whole problem at all, but secondly, we know that there are, of course, Europe-wide differences. There are already differences between Italy, Germany and Austria. In Italy, one just goes out. In Germany, the total ban on smoking was tilted by the Constitutional Court, Landesverfassungshof, because the hostess was able to prove that this meant virtually 20 percent less turnover, which was dangerous to many. That's why the total ban on smoking was tilted there. That is why, I believe, the Austrian step forward is an important step. However, I am not at all blue-eyed, I am sure that the trend from the EU side, but also the social trend actually goes towards non-smoking. As a doctor, I'll tell you: That's quite the way it is.

I would like to point out that Austria was one of the first countries to have written a ban on smoking for the public covered area as a law. The manko or problem was that we had no sanctions in this area. Only signs were required, but we had no means of verifying whether the ban on smoking is being observed in public covered areas or not. However, with this new law, we have been able to ensure that this area is also subject to sanctions for the first time. I believe that we have not only enabled non-smokers protection and protection against passive smoking, but I believe that we have created as high a flexibility as possible for gastronomy in the sense of voluntary smoking. In view of this, I believe that it was important and important to achieve equality between these two areas, and we have made this equality possible through this law. That is, total bans obviously have a special appeal. The formula is therefore: far-reaching protection of non-smokers on the one hand, especially workers, but self-responsibility of the host and guests on the other hand is certainly a very promising one. Mrs Bundesminister, I thank you very much for this law, which takes particular account of the health aspect. The state must respect freedom of choice and self-determination based on self-reliance if it does not dare to ban smoking completely. Therefore, a general ban on smoking in all locals is difficult to implement in principle and, of course, for economic reasons. We should not necessarily look at other countries, we should seek our own creative solutions in the sense of non-smokers protection and peace in society. The Minister of Foreign Affairs has been very careful and skillful. Thank you very much! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Now there is a tailor-made and unmistakable Rules of Procedure with the highest principle: A non-smoker should be able to stay in a gastronomy room unlit. This solution is a guarantee and a prerequisite for a peaceful co-operation. The solution is also in line with the Government Convention. There have been some points in the Government Convention over the last two years. Ladies and gentlemen, I have the feeling that the number of those who say this law goes too far is now higher than the number of those who say it goes too little far. This is proof to me that this law is also a good law from a health point of view. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-09-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00067_d8e14600	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I would like to make a few fundamental comments on the relationship between policy and justice in my opinion on this request. At the beginning of 2007, I took over the department with the very urgent task of the government programme to push ahead with the implementation of the criminal process reform, so that its entry into force with 1. January 2008 is secured. I have really devoted myself fully to this objective, because I believe that this reform can achieve decisive improvements in the role division between criminal police, prosecutors and court on the one hand and the right to understand, participate and control of accused and victims on the other. This system of mutual control is based on a very strong pillar, namely the independence of justice, which I fully regard as a particularly protective asset of Austrian society. I should also like to remind you that, as a result of my initiative, this National Council has stated in the Constitution that the Public Prosecutorship is a part of jurisdiction and is therefore, in particular, bound to the requirements of unimpeded, objective conduct of proceedings. [[Abg. May. Brigid Weinzinger: That would be it!]] In this sense, I have always known how to avoid even the mere appearance of an ever-consuming influence on the decisions of jurisdiction and prosecutors, but at the same time to comply with all the information obligations to the law-making bodies. It was therefore also a special concern for me to make the exercise of the law of law so transparent that it does not involve the smell of political influence on criminal proceedings. The rules adopted by this National Council in the context of the Monitoring Regulation on Criminal Procedure Reform also ensure that any guidance on the files is to be taken into account and is therefore subject to the case-law of the parties to the proceedings. I am therefore saying this because today we are in a situation where, regardless of the decisions of the independent judicial system, the claim of an abusive prosecution is set out in order to adopt a resolution whose point 1 can only be interpreted in such a way that the National Council, in fact, in a single case, assumes that the Article 287a of the Criminal Code is applied by law. In today’s issue of the “Salzburger Nachrichten”, Andreas Koller has noted in a slightly different context whether it would actually be useful to vote on what punishment would be appropriate in a particular case. I believe that this is at the heart of the case, because I cannot accept that, during a pending judicial procedure, the decisions of the courts of appeal are to be prejudiced. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Indeed, an indisputable advantage of the reform of criminal proceedings lies in the fact that allegations of disproportionate use of force against police bodies are also examined in the context of a judicial procedure. I think it would be a sign of respect for independent justice to wait for these decisions and to give the judicial authority confidence here. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. May. Brigid Weinzinger: That's why we must not change laws?]] Of course, I would like to comply with my obligation to inform the House, which is why, without any comment on the law and proportionality, I can report on the course of the procedure: ==References== In November 2006, the public prosecutor's office in Wiener Neustadt filed an advertisement of the State Office for Constitutional Protection and Anti-Terrorism in Lower Austria against unknown perpetrators for damage to property. ==References== ==References== On May 1, 2007, further advertisements were paid against the still to be investigated accused of material injury to the Tatorts Perchtoldsdorf and Vienna. As a result, the Vienna Neustadt Procuratorate repeatedly filed follow-up documents, including those of the Vienna Criminal Directorate, showing the relevant facts by the police first because of criminal association, § 287 StGB, later because of criminal organisation, § 278a StGB. On 21 and 22. In May 2008, ten defendants were arrested and numerous searches of places and objects were carried out in execution of the judicial orders. The competent judge of the Landesgericht Wiener Neustadt imposed the arrest on the arrested for the risk of evasion and crime. ==References== ==References====External links== In July 2008, the Court of First Instance ordered the continuation of the arrest on the grounds of past detention. The decisions in question set out in detail the facts determining the urgency of the case, which are well justified in detail for both the cause of the risk of evasion and those of the risk of action and the proportionality. According to the information available to me, some defendants have also brought an action against the arrest and almost all the defendants against the continuation of the arrest, as well as some accused and affected persons against individual investigation and forced action, such as the search of apartments. The Oberlandesgericht Wien will have to decide on these appeals. The investigation procedure covers not only § 278a StGB, but also other criminal offences. The limitation of the case-law is based on § 51 para. 2 last sentence of the criminal order. Thereafter, the case may be limited before the investigation procedure is terminated in so far as it is of particular concern that the purpose of the investigations would be jeopardised by an immediate notice of certain documents. However, if the defendant is in detention, a restriction of the case-law regarding documents which may be relevant for the assessment of the suspect or the grounds of detention shall be inadmissible after the arrest has been imposed. Because of the alleged violation of subjective rights, several defendants objected to legal infringement according to § 106 para. 1 StPO, which has not yet been decided by the Court of First Instance. According to the information available to me, only a few documents are currently excluded from the file view. Ladies and gentlemen, now generally to the § 278a Criminal Code. Animal welfare is also an important concern to me. I feel high regard for everyone who is active in this area, for example, to discover illegal and animal torture activities and practices. However, no more honourable motive can provide a justification for serious crimes if this is not explicitly provided for in the law. [[Congratulations to the ÖVP and to Mr Dr. Fichtenbauer and Mr Schalle.]] [[Abg. May. Brigid Weinzinger: What is a serious crime?]] I would also like to refer you to a comparison with § 278c Abs. 3 to draw attention to the fact that an act is not considered to be a terrorist crime if it is aimed at the exercise of human rights. Peaceful protests up to measures that enable the competent institutions to pursue animal torture activities are certainly covered by the fundamental rights of freedom of expression and freedom of assembly. [[Abg. May. Brigid Weinzinger: demonstrations, for example!]] Animal welfare may therefore actively participate in protests and blockades or similar, but not continue to harm foreign property or commit other crimes. [[Congratulation of the Abg. New builder.]] On the other hand, it is clear from the fact that the crimes listed there, such as severe need or serious damage to property, are also punishable if they serve a worthy purpose in themselves. I would like you to take into account that any other view would constitute a fundamental change in the principle that violence is reserved for the exercise of state authority. [[Abg. May. Brigid Weinzinger: Call me a serious crime, Mrs. Minister!]] I should also like to point out in the history of this case-law that it was introduced into the Criminal Code by an own-initiative request from the National Council in 1993. The National Council took into account the criticism of the then very open-minded scope, which was merely based on the establishment of an organisation aimed at the continued conduct of certain crimes in the course of the deliberations on the Criminal Law Change Act 1996. On the other hand, the current version of this criminal provision, which is ultimately due to this amendment, is far more narrow, both in terms of the underlying offences and in terms of the degree of organisation required. I should like to quote a few examples from the case law of the Supreme Court, which is to be understood by the already mentioned requirement to protect against law enforcement measures. This includes the establishment or management of a sham company for the purpose of camouflage, the frequent change of mobile phones used by the members with value cards, the use of code words in telephone calls and counter-observation. These are examples of when the crime of shielding law enforcement is to be accepted. I would also like to point out that these and related provisions have been implemented in the implementation of international legal obligations, in particular in the implementation of the Convention on the control of organised crime. The renewed criminal code contains a number of protection provisions, such as the command of law, proportionality and legal hearing. The interested parties have the opportunity to request the decision of the independent court on alleged infringements of these rights. This includes, as mentioned above, § 51 criminal proceedings order, according to which the defendant has the right in principle to take into account the results of the investigation and the main proceedings of the criminal police, the prosecutor's office and the court. In other debates, I had the opportunity to point out that the reform of the criminal procedure is a very far-reaching reform project, that, as well as we could, we have taken precautions for a good functioning, but we have also seen the need to be scientifically accompanied by the effectiveness of the new criminal procedure system. This scientific accompaniment includes monitoring, evaluating the extent to which the prosecutors play their new role. In this sense, at any rate, a point that you also address in your address is to be regarded as already guided in the way. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-09-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00067_d8e16669	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Back to the Drugs Act and the Health and Food Safety Act. Under current law, the cultivation of plants for the production of a drug is prohibited in Austria. Certain institutions or institutions which do so for scientific purposes are excluded. Also the cultivation of plants of the genus Cannabis – is known – for the production of substances for the purpose of pharmaceutical production is not allowed, as these substances are also classified as addictions. My previous speakers were mainly concerned with the reporting obligations of the District Administration Authority as a health authority to the Federal Ministry of Health, Family and Youth, as well as with the topics of advertising and data protection. As so often, opinions are shared. However, the fact is that it is basically a change to the Drugs Act, an adaptation to European and national law. Under the supervision of the Federal Ministry of Health, Family and Youth, the Austrian Agency for Health and Food Safety GmbH is to be entrusted with the task of using the active substances derived from cannabis plants not only for pharmaceutical research but also for the production of industrial medicinal products. The healing effect of the cannabis plant has been known for several centuries. Substances derived from this plant have some pain-killing and debilitating effects and are used by doctors in many areas – chemotherapy, HIV, spastics, multiple sclerosis, spinal cord disease and so on. In my opinion, therefore, the cultivation of the plant of the genus Cannabis is to be welcomed in principle in order to obtain substances for the production of medicinal products. We will agree.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-09-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00067_d8e19581	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen of the House! We are now dealing with two requests. The first concerns the examination of the dedicated use of the care allowance by health managers. It is a matter of encouraging a comprehensive use of health managers to verify the appropriate use of the care allowance. This is justified by the fact that this is not sufficient at the moment. I must say, Mr Hofer, that we do not think this is correct because quality controls are very likely to take place, through the farmers' social insurance institution, which acts as a centre of competence for social security institutions. We believe that this competence centre, with these quality tests, is sufficient. That is why I have already said in committee that we will not agree to this request. The second request deals with care benefits abroad. It calls on the government to take all necessary steps to ensure that the care allowance rests with foreign residents in the future. This is due to the fact that the verification abroad is very difficult and that the administration in the liaison bodies is very difficult to implement. I must tell you that this is not possible because the care benefit is an insurance benefit, a benefit based on deposits. In other words, people have acquired entitlements, and these benefits are paid abroad only if the people in need of care have acquired this claim domestically. There is also an ECJ judgment, which states that it is simply not possible for foreign relations to rest. That is why we will not vote in favour of this request. I would like to make a few comments on the care allowance and even the care debate because I think that this issue will continue to concern us for a very long time. It is an issue that is being discussed very emotionally, because it concerns a lot of people. I believe that the approach of Federal Minister Buchinger to find a great solution here and not only to seek small solutions on time, but to think about many ways in the care sector is the right one. I also think that it would be very, very important to increase the amount of care, for which there is the novelle of the Federal Care Act, because it would really be an extremely important contribution that would relieve people very much. I also see the new classification of people with dementia and disabled children and young people as a whole, very important point. I am very sorry that the ÖVP does not go along with this so important law. The moment one facilitates access to the labour market for migrants, they have a labour income, i.e. they pay income tax, they pay social security contributions, wage tax and need fewer social transfers. Therefore, the most logical thing would be to go here and say: Those who are legally resident in Austria automatically have legal access to the labour market. This is what the social system could clearly relieve quickly. And that should apply at least to refugees who do not come voluntarily. [[Abg. Strache: Maybe for these Georgian asylum seekers who are on their way across Austria as a criminal!]] It is humane to force people to do anything for years, to wait for them to do anything, and at the same time to regret that they have to pay for basic care. We have another study, Mrs Haubner. That would also be something for you, Mr Strache, if you would like to read something in between. The Bertelsmann study calculates for Germany what the costs of non-inclusion are, wherever the state prevents or does not provide sufficient support, especially the lack of integration into the labour market. For Germany it is according to the calculation model, three are presented, between 12 and 15 billion € per year. In Austria, the situation is certainly about the same, that is, our value will be about one tenth. That means we have enormous costs by hindering integration. It should therefore be the logical consequence: Above all, we need improved integration, improved access to the labour market for migrants. The last thing we need is a study with a recognizable intention, which the liberals would like to order for the election campaign.

Point Report by the Committee on Consumer Protection on amendment 827/A(E) by Mr Johann Rädler, Mr Mag. Johann Maier, Sigisbert Dolinschek, colleagues on the revision of the European Cosmetics Regulation (654 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-09-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00067_d8e22703	We're going to the 18th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. The first speaker to speak is Mr Steier. 3 minutes speaking time. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-09-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00067_d8e22730	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The improvement of the safety of cosmetics is at the heart of the 827/A application. On the occasion of the revision of European legislation on cosmetic products, the Cosmetics Directive 1976 is to be replaced by a single regulation with its numerous innovations and national legislation. Higher product safety requirements and simplified rules for reporting new cosmetic products are planned. This is a good reason to call for more precise formulations and obligations to ensure legal certainty for manufacturers, but especially for customers. Ladies and gentlemen, The term “cosmetics” includes not only makeups or perfumes, but also millions of products used for personal hygiene, such as dental care, shampoos or soaps. New insights, products and material applications make it necessary to adapt the existing rules in the sense of consumers. The application therefore proposes, inter alia, that the introduction of minimum standards does not lead to a reduction in the previous levels and that the limitation of the so-called CMR substances, which include carcinogenic, mutagenic and reproductive toxic substances, remain maintained. It is particularly important to protect consumers from possible health risks by falsifying cosmetics, because from 2006 to 2007, cosmetic products seized by customs services at the EU's external borders increased by 264 percent. In short, I would like to highlight a subject that plays a very important role in the context of cosmetics, but also in general, of nanotechnology. It is used in various consumer products and is increasingly used. The market for nano products grows daily and very strong. Substances with completely new properties arise. In the cosmetics area, for example, “Nanos” can be found in day creams with UV protection, tooth creams and so on. However, risk research on nanoparticles is still in its infancy. This does not prevent health risks – for example, sprays with nanotechnology. In our opinion, particular attention must be paid to health problems in cosmetics. Ladies and gentlemen! Although nanomaterials are contained in more and more products, the Austrian consumers know far too little about nanotechnology, their use and their potential risks. It is therefore all the more important that the topic of nanotechnology in cosmetics and foodstuffs be taken up at national level in cooperation with the AGES, thus continuing to provide more space for consumer protection. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-09-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00067_d8e22789	As the next speaker, I would ask Mrs Grander to speak. 2 minutes of voluntary time limit. – Mrs Grander is not present. The next speaker is Mrs Hradecsni. I wish you every time: 3 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-09-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00067_d8e22813	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Yes, we also support this request because, as a result of the recasting of the European Cosmetics Regulation, there is a need to continue to be a strict restriction on CMR-containing substances. The establishment of a rapid warning system at EU level also seems extremely important to us so that consumers are informed as soon as possible of dangerous and non-traffic cosmetics. However, this regulation does not go far enough, precisely in the area which the colleague has already mentioned, namely nanotechnology. There is no labelling obligation, and consumers do not know whether they are buying a product that includes nanoparticles. – This is a particular problem in the summer in the area of sunscreens, which is especially applied to children. There is still far too little risk research in this area, especially in Austria. We have therefore proposed a green 5-point programme on nanotechnology, which would include: the preparation of an Austrian Nanotechnology Action Plan; a moratorium for food and cosmetic products, derived from the precautionary principle; and, more importantly, the increase in risk research funding to at least 5 percent of nanotechnology funding; and the commitment to the Code of Conduct of the EU Commission and the publication of the Austrian opinion. In the Consumer Committee, we discussed on-goingly that a subcommittee should be set up in the Research Committee, which should deal with this urgent issue. Now, with the resolution of the National Council, we are facing the problem that this will delay for a further month. However, it would be very urgent to take action at national level in the field of nanotechnology.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-09-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00067_d8e23146	We're coming to the 19th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Spindelberger was the first speaker to speak. 3 minutes desired speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-09-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00067_d8e23174	Madam President. Federal Minister! As everyone knows, roaming – i.e. the English term for “reverse strips” – is the name for the use of a foreign network in mobile communications, but also on the Internet. It is undisputed that we have to deal with major problems in this context, because the radio waves do not stick to the federal boundaries. Therefore, we experience both mobile phones and internet users who live or leave border areas, sometimes evil surprises. Internet surfing abroad is usually more expensive than in Austria. Moreover, if we stick to the issue of the Internet, nor do we show users the change to a foreign network or the new, much more expensive cost structure resulting from it. In these cases, it is not rare that on-line monthly bills are totally surprising in part, even several thousand euros. Consumerists of the Chamber of Workers and the VKI confirm that such victims are not very rare, because the problem is that when you surf the Internet, the change to another network – whether abroad or in border areas – takes place without information, and therefore the Internet users usually do not know whether they are in a foreign network, which is exactly what leads to these high roaming charges that are unwitting. Such a situation, as one sees in tomorrow’s “curier”, has led to the fact that there is now a well-directional judgment in the field of roaming fees for discussions conducted domestically, which could also become a precedent for Internet users in the future. Tomorrow’s “Kurier” says that a reasonable user of a mobile phone had to expect to call at a higher rate abroad, but not at an international rate. Ladies and gentlemen! This action by a network operator was also rejected for the reason that there was no information SMS in this context that one was back in a foreign network. But to return to the internet users who are at the heart of our request: In our joint five-party application, we would like to ask the relevant members of the Austrian Federal Government to work at EU level to ensure that Internetsurfing, in general, when changing the provider network, automatically sends a message to the Internet user – whether in the form of text messages, popups or e-mails – that informs the user about the new situation and the costs incurred. I would ask you, Minister, to make a strong effort in this regard in Brussels to make a similar arrangement, as it already exists for mobile phone users, for Internet users.

The Treaty between the Republic of Austria and the Republic of Croatia on police cooperation will directly benefit security in Austria. This cooperation with the Croatian police shall include, in particular, the following measures: Joint security analyses, increased obligation to provide police assistance, cooperation in education and training, cooperation on anti-corruption, support for police-controlled transports, new empowerment for cross-border police intervention such as cross-border hidden investigation and common strips, facilitating the establishment of joint investigation groups, cooperation in the field of witness protection, regulation of the posting of officials to contact services operated by the other Contracting State with third countries. This model has been adopted in one way by the model that we are already very successful in maintaining with our neighbours and that we have also brought into the European Union, namely that it will be examined whether it will also be used across the EU. It is now a bilateral treaty, which aims to optimise the fight against crime across borders. I would like to thank the House for its approval of this Treaty.
